The function evaluate_sequence: 
```

sequence = [max(0, x) for x in sequence]

```

Since objective depends on c = a*a (nonnegative). For normalized sum=1. The objective L = max c. We can use subgradient descent:

At current a, compute c. Let active set = indices k where c_k >= L - ε (near max). Gradient of L (a smooth approximation) ≈ 2 * ∑_{active w_k} shift(a, k) / num. Where shift(a,k)_m = a_{k-m} if in range else 0. Then update a = a - step * grad. Project to simplex: a = max(a,0); a /= sum(a). This reduces mass at positions that participate in active sums. However shifting mass from a position decreases all sums involving it; good. But it may create new peaks. Step size important.

We can also use lignesearch: try multiple step sizes e.g., 0.01,0.05,0.1 on L1? Since sum changes, normalize.

Active weights: if multiple peaks, weight by (c_k - secondmax + small)^? Use all near max equally or overweight exact max.

Alternative gradient for smoothed max: c^T exp(T c) / sum exp. Use log-sum-exp gradient: grad = 2 * ∑ p_k shift(a,k), where p_k=exp(T(c_k-Lmax))/Z. We can implement this and use L-BFGS? Simpler: fixed T iterate with subgradient.

3. Active set constrained minimization (Frank-Wolfe style):
At each c, choose k with max. To reduce c_k, we need decrease products a_i a_{k-i}. We can move mass from positions involved in that sum to positions not involved in max sums. For a single active k, the gradient vector is 2*reverse window. We can formulate a linearized feasible direction that decreases c_k while keeping sum: choose perturbation d = -P_{simplex} grad + (constant) to keep sum? Projected gradient does that.

Let's design an iterative local optimizer:

Function `optimize_sequence(a, deadline)`:
- n fixed.
- best_a,a_best,current.
- Use normalized a.
- For stage T in [1,5,20,100?]: Actually if using subgradient max, no T.
- Step schedule: random perturbations to escape.

Algorithm loop:
```
best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score(a)
it=0
while time < deadline-end:
    if it % 20 ==0: random_restart/mutation? maybe small
    c = fft_conv(a)
    maxc = c.max()
    score=2*n*maxc
    if score<best: update
    # choose active p
    threshold = maxc - tol*maxc
    active = np.nonzero(c>=threshold)[0]
    if len(active) small and direct:
       grad = np.zeros(n)
       for k in active:
          if k < n: grad[:k+1] += a[k::-1]
          else:
             m0 = k-n+1; mlen = n-m0
             grad[m0:] += a[k-m0::-1]? Need correct.
    else:
       p = c.copy(); p[p<threshold]=0; p/=p.sum() # or softmax
       grad = fft_gradient(a,p)
    # gradient descent with line search on simplex
    # Try several step sizes and random directions?
    for step in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5] maybe plus random:
        new = a - step * grad / (grad.max() or 1) ? 
    # Better: use relative step: step_lambda = fraction of active mass? 
```

Need define step scaling. grad magnitude varies. We can normalize grad by its max or L1. Since a values ~1/n, c~1/n. For active max, grad components in window =2*a_{k-m}; values up to 2 max_a. Norm maybe O(sqrt(window)*max_a). Step should be dimensionless. If we do a_new = a - α * grad / (max_a) maybe. Let `scale = max(a)`? For a~1/n, max_a maybe ~5/n. grad max ~2max_a. Normalize by grad.max(). Then step α in [0,1] moves a by at most α max_a. That seems reasonable. Use α=0.05-0.3. But if gradient sparse, only one component changes a lot, which might be good. Need avoid negative.

Alternatively use perturbation direction d = -grad (where nonzero). Project to simplex: Let min_neg = -α*grad.max(); a+... then clip. Renormalize. Step α=0.01 maybe.

Line search: We can evaluate candidate score with FFT (cheap) and accept if lower. If none, try smaller α. Use multiple random α.

But gradient of max may point to reducing the max coefficient but can increase others. Simulated annealing: accept worse with probability exp((old-new)/temperature) maybe.

Could use a "pushing" operation: For the worst coefficient k, we want to reduce ∑ a_i a_{k-i}. One effective way: take mass from one side of the window and move to another position not in many active windows. For example, pick m in support of grad (i.e., m and k-m pair), transfer mass from m to some j where active involvement low. This is coordinate update. We can implement:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _next_fast_len(n):
    if n <= 1: return 1
    p=1
    while p < n: p*=2
    # maybe use 2^a 3^b 5^c? numpy fastest powers of 2 ok.
    # Could implement next p^a q^b with p in (2,3,5) to reduce.
```
Better implement `next_fast_len` trying primes [2,3,5] to reduce N for 9611: 9611 next 2^14=16384; but 2*? 3^? 3^9=19683, 5^6=15625. 16384 okay. For 14416, 16384. Good.

```
def fft_conv_full(a):
    n=len(a); N=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)
    # due to roundoff, maybe small negative; take first 2n-1
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    if c.size and c.min() < 0: c=np.maximum(c,0)
    return c
```
For speed, cache N and maybe A? a changes.

```
def gradient_from_weights(a, p, Ngrad=None):

    ar=np.ascontiguousarray(a[:0:-1])? Need ar length n.

    R=np.fft.rfft(ar, N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(P*R, N)
    # conv index n-1..2n-2 length n
```

```
def gradient_direct(a, active, weights=None):
    n=len(a); g=np.zeros(n)
    for idx,k in enumerate(active):
       w=weights[idx] if weights else 1
       if k < n:
          seg_len=k+1
          g[:seg_len] += w * a[k::-1]
       else:
          m0=k-n+1
          seg_len=n-m0
          g[m0:] += w * a[k-m0::-1]
    return 2*g
```

```
def spectral_reflect(a):
    n=len(a); N=fft_len(2n-1)
    A=rfft(a,N)
    for it in range(5):
       c=ifft(A*A); clip min0; csum=c.sum()
       c_clip=np.minimum(c, c.max()*0.9) # reduce peaks
       c_clip *= 1.0/c_clip.sum()
       C=rfft(c_clip,N)
       # set A_new by interpolation to A: A_new = A * sqrt(|C|/|A^2|) maybe
       oldC=A*A
       ratio=np.sqrt(np.abs(C)/np.maximum(np.abs(oldC),1e-300))
       A_new=A*ratio
       a_try=irfft(A_new,N)[:2n-1]; # Not a, A is sqrt? Wait A_new is still A, time coefficients a_new=irfft(A_new)
       a_try=irfft(A_new,N)[:n]? Actually a time coefficients are ifft(A), first n values, rest zero? If A changed, a may have support full N. We only keep first n and clip.
       proj = np.maximum(a_try[:n],0); normalize
    return best
```

```
       L=c.max()

       # choose direct if p is sparse: count p where >1e-3
       if np.count_nonzero(p>1e-3) < 20: grad=direct
       else: grad=fft_gradient
       gdot = np.dot(a, grad)
       grad_w = a * (grad - gdot)
       # update adam
       t+=1
       m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*grad_w
       v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*(grad_w*grad_w)
       mhat=m/(1-beta1**t); vhat=v/(1-beta2**t)
       # try steps
       best_cand=a; best_sc=fft_score(c)
       for lr in step_sizes:
           dw=mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
           if max(abs(dw)) huge: scale
           w_try=w + lr*dw
           a_try=softmax(w_try)
           sc=fft_score(a_try)
           if sc<best_sc: ...
       if accepted:
           w=w_try; a=a_try; update best
       else:
           # occasionally add noise
           if random: add small gaussian to w, normalize
```

`fft_score(a, N=None, return_c=False)`:
```
def fft_score(a, N=None, return_c=False):
    n=len(a)
    if N is None: N=next_fast_len(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    if c.size:
       if c.min()<0: c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    mx=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    sc=2.0*n*mx
    if return_c: return sc,c
    return sc
```
If `a` is not C contiguous, okay.

`fft_gradient(a, p, Ng=None)`:
```
def fft_gradient(a, p, Ng=None):
    n=len(a)
    if Ng is None: Ng=next_fast_len(3*n-2)
    ar=np.ascontiguousarray(a[::-1])
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, Ng)
    R=np.fft.rfft(ar, Ng)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(P*R, Ng)
    return 2.0*conv[n-1:2*n-1] # length n? n-1 to n-1+n exclusive? slice n-1 : n-1+n = 2n-1.
```

```
   for s,w in zip(active,weights):
      if s < n:
```

```
def next_fast_len(m):
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<=1
    return p
```

```
def exact_score_array(a):
    if a.size==0: return inf
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=0: return inf
    b=np.convolve(a, a)
    mb=np.max(b)
    return float(2*a.size*mb/(s*s))
```

Random block in log space:
```
def random_log_candidate_from_w(w, rng, n, sig=0.1, blocks=1):
    w_try = w.copy()
    for _ in range(blocks):
       width = rng.integers(max(2,n//32), max(3,n//4))
       start = rng.integers(0, n-width)
       w_try[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0, sig, width)
    return log_softmax(w_try)
```

Function `as_valid_sequence(obj)`:
```
def as_valid_sequence(obj):
    if obj is None: return None
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
       return None
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s<=0: return None
    return arr/s
```

```
    return None
```

```
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start+max(0,budget_s-12)
```

```
def perform_cross_update(f, a_target, N, phase, beta=20, lr=0.05, iters=20, rng=None):
    b = a_target.copy()
    w = log...
    F = rfft(f,N); FR = phase*conj(F)
    m=zeros; v=zeros; t=0
    for it in range(iters):
       B=rfft(b,N); h=irfft(F*B,N)[:2n-1]; L=max
       p=make_weights(h,beta)
       P=rfft(p,N); grad=irfft(P*FR,N)[n-1:2n-1]
       gw = b*(grad - dot(b,grad))
       update ...
       w_try = w - lr*dw; b=softmax
    return b
```
No factor 2. For linear objective, gradient of max is shift(f) (not 2). Good.

Let's formulate final search as:
- Current `f` (sequence for self objective) and `b` (subproblem variable).
- Main loop:
   * If time segment: optimize b toward min max(f*b) for `inner_steps` (e.g., 3-10).
   * Evaluate possible updates of f: the optimized b and convex combinations with old f.
   * If f improves, update.
   * If stagnation, use self-gradient step on f or random perturbation.
This is robust.

Potential issue: If `f` is updated to a mix, the self objective changes. The subproblem b was optimized for old f; may not be optimized for new f but warm.

We need track best self score. `f_score` is FFT score of current f. `best` separate.

Let's write specific helper functions:
`fft_score_only(a,N)`: rfft+irfft max.
`fft_score_A(a,N,Cut)`: returns A,c,score.
`make_weights(c,beta)`.
`softmax(w)`.
`log_of(a)`.

`update_b(fF,fFR,b,w,m,v,ite,beta,lr,phase,N)`: This is not easy top-level without many params. We can write a helper that does one inner step and returns new b,w,m,v,t.
```
def cross_inner_step(F, FR, b, w, m, v, t, beta, lr, N):
    n = len(b)
    B = np.fft.rfft(b, N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(F * B, N)
    h = h[:2*n-1]
    if h.min() < 0: h = np.maximum(h,0)
    L = float(np.max(h))
    p = make_weights(h,beta)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    grad = np.fft.irfft(P * FR, N)[n-1:2*n-1]
    grad_w = b * (grad - np.dot(b, grad))
    # Adam update
    t +=1
    m = 0.9*m +0.1*grad_w
    v = 0.999*v +0.001*(grad_w*grad_w)
    mhat = m/(1-0.9**t)
    vhat = v/(1-0.999**t)
    dw = mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
    dw = np.clip(dw,-10,10)
    w_try = w - lr*dw
    w_try -= np.max(w_try)
    b_try = np.exp(w_try); s=b_try.sum(); b_try/=s
    return b_try, w_try, m, v, t
```
No factor 2. But if we use smoothed objective with beta, gradient of (1/beta)logsumexp(beta h) is ∑p grad, same. Good.

Self gradient step helper:
```
def self_gradient_update(a, A, c, w, m, v, t, beta, lr, N, phase):
   p=make_weights(c,beta)
   P=rfft(p,N); FR=phase*conj(A); conv=irfft(P*FR,N); grad=2*conv[n-1:2n-1]
   ... grad_w, Adam, w_try = w - lr*dw, a_try
```
We can reuse same Adam update logic via helper `adam_log_step(grad_w, w, m, v, t, lr)` returning candidate w,m,v,t. But no closures.
```
def adam_step_log(grad_w, w, m, v, t, lr, beta1=0.9,beta2=0.999):
    m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*grad_w
    v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*(grad_w*grad_w)
    mhat = m/(1-beta1**t)
    vhat = v/(1-beta2**t)
    dw = mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
    dw = np.clip(dw,-10,10)
    w_new = w - lr*dw
    w_new -= np.max(w_new)
    e=np.exp(w_new); a=e/e.sum()
    return a,w_new,m,v
```
Need t increment. Return t+1. But m,v arrays large; do we create copies every call? In Python, `m = beta1*m +...` creates new arrays; okay but memory/time. To avoid copies maybe but fine. n small. However many calls, array allocations overhead. Could be okay.

But if we reassign m,v in helper and return, okay. No in-place? Could use in-place to reduce allocation? We can implement in place:
```
m *= beta1; m += (1-beta1)*grad_w
v *= beta2; v += (1-beta2)*grad_w*grad_w
```
But `grad_w*grad_w` allocation. Use `gw2 = grad_w*grad_w`. Fine.

Make sure arrays not modified unexpectedly.

```
def mix_seq(x,y,t):
   z = (1-t)*x + t*y
   return z/z.sum()
```

```
def run_search(...):
    f = a_best
    f_score = score_best
    b = f.copy()
    w_b = log(b); m_b=zeros; v_b=zeros; tb=0
    F = rfft(f,N); FR=phase*conj(F)
    beta_cross=10; lr_cross=0.1
    beta_self=5; lr_self=0.05
    inner_count=0

    mode_toggle=0
    while time<deadline:

       for _ in range(inner):
           b,w_b,m_b,v_b,tb = cross_inner_step(F,FR,b,w_b,m_b,v_b,tb,beta_cross,lr_cross,N)
       # evaluate candidate updates for f
       candidates = []
       # maybe current b
       cand_scores = []

           if rng.random()<0.5:
               b=f.copy(); w_b=log(b); reset m_b
           else:
               w_b=log(b) # if b finite
       else:
           stall += 1
           if stall > 20:

               A,c,s... p=make_weights(c,beta_self)

       # top add if global improved
```

Need `w_f` for self gradient. Maintain current f log w_f. When f updated via mix, recompute w_f = log(f). For self gradient, use w_f.

In cross inner step, b variable separate. If we update f to b or mix, we might set b=f (reset) or keep optimized b. Keep optimized b can be good; but w_b should correspond to b. If we choose mix as f, b remains old optimized solution (not mix). That's okay; subproblem variable can start from old b for new f. But if old b has self score not good, no problem. For evaluating mix, t=1 means f=b. If we update f to mix not b, b still old. Good.

However, F,FR for new f. b remains old feasible? okay.

Maybe we should initialize b with random diverse not just f to avoid stuck. Could occasionally reset b to random. Add if stall high: `b = random_log_candidate(f, sig=0.5)`.

Cross subproblem inner step uses Adam state; if b resets, reset m,v.

Beta cross schedule: start 5, increase every 200 outer or stall: [5,10,20,50,100]. Since h max scale, beta relative.

This cross phase might be very effective. Let's include random moves as fallback.

Time: Each inner cross step uses h computation via F*B. But `F` is fixed; B rfft. P rfft. 2 rffts + 2 irffts. If 3 inner per outer and 3 candidate evals (each 1 rfft+irfft), total ~ (3*4 +3*2)=18 FFT per outer. If outer 10000, 180k FFT. Maybe too many. Need tune. For N=16384, Python FFT maybe 0.2ms? 180k*0.2=36s? Actually maybe 1ms =>180s. Okay.

We can reduce candidates: evaluate only t=0.5 and t=1 maybe 2. If improving, try t=0.2? Simpler 2. inner=2. Total 2*4+2*2=12 FFT. Good.

Use time check inside inner? Not necessary.

Could optimize cross inner by reusing B? b changes each inner, no.

Potential problem: The cross subproblem optimum b might be multiple; b can become very sparse causing self score high; mix with f avoids. If no mix improves, use self-gradient.

`make_weights`:
```
def make_weights(c,beta):
    L=float(np.max(c))
    if L <= 0 or not finite: idx=argmax; p...
    x=(c-L)/L
    e=np.exp(np.clip(beta*x, -745, 0))
    s=e.sum()
    if s==0 or not finite: p=zeros; p[argmax(c)]=1
    else p=e/s
    return p
```
If beta*x can be positive due to rounding? L max so x≤0; exp <=1. Good.

`cross_inner_step`:
```
def cross_inner_step(F, FR, b, w, m, v, t, beta, lr, N):
    n=len(b)
    B=np.fft.rfft(b,N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(F*B,N)
    h=h[:2*n-1]
    if h.min()<0: h=np.maximum(h,0)
    L=float(np.max(h))
    p=make_weights(h,beta)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    grad=np.fft.irfft(P*FR,N)
    grad=grad[n-1:2*n-1]
    grad_w=b*(grad - np.dot(b,grad))
    a_new, w_new, m_new, v_new, t_new = adam_log_step(grad_w,w,m,v,t,lr)
    return a_new,w_new,m_new,v_new,t_new
```
No factor 2.

`adam_log_step`:
```
def adam_log_step(grad_w, w, m, v, t, lr, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.999):
    t = t+1
    m = m*beta1 + grad_w*(1-beta1)
    v = v*beta2 + (grad_w*grad_w)*(1-beta2)
    mhat = m/(1-beta1**t)
    vhat = v/(1-beta2**t)
    dw = mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
    maxdw = float(np.max(np.abs(dw)))
    if not finite or maxdw ==0: dw=np.zeros_like(dw)
    else: dw = np.clip(dw, -10, 10)
    w_new = w - lr*dw
    w_new = w_new - np.max(w_new)
    a = sequence_from_log_weights(w_new)
    return a,w_new,m,v,t
```
Potential if `t` large, `1-beta1**t` underflows? beta1**t ->0 eventually, okay. For t up to 1e6, fine. `beta2**t` maybe 0.

`self_gradient_candidate` similar but returns candidate and updated states for f. We can compute inside run_search to use A. But helper:
```
def self_gradient_candidate(f, A, c, w, m, v, t, beta, lr, N, phase):
    p=make_weights(c,beta)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    FR=phase*np.conjugate(A)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(P*FR,N)
    grad=2.0*conv[n-1:2*n-1]
    grad_w=f*(grad - np.dot(f,grad))
    return adam_log_step(...)
```
Need n = len(f). Good.

`random_log_candidate`:
```
def random_log_candidate(w, rng, n, sig=0.1, blocks=1):
    w_try = w.copy()
    for _ in range(blocks):
       width = int(rng.integers(max(2, n//64), max(3, n//8)))
       if width >= n: width=n
       start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
       if rng.random() < 0.2:
           w_try[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0.0, max(0.05, sig*0.5), size=width)
       else:
           w_try[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=width)
    return sequence_from_log_weights(w_try - np.max(w_try))
```
Maybe also global noise.

`swap_blocks_candidate`:
```
def swap_blocks_candidate(a, rng, n):
    b=a.copy()
    w1=int(rng.integers(2, max(3,n//10+1)))
    w2=w1
    i=int(rng.integers(0,n-w1+1)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n-w2+1))
    tmp=b[i:i+w1].copy(); b[i:i+w1]=b[j:j+w2]; b[j:j+w2]=tmp
    s=b.sum(); if s>0: b/=s
    return b
```
Probability low.

```
def run_search(a_start, seed=42, budget_s=2400.0):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 15.0)
    f = normalize_nonneg(a_start)
    n = f.size
    if n ==0: fallback
    N = next_fast_len(3*n - 2)
    phase = np.exp(-2j*np.pi*np.arange(N//2+1)*(n-1)/float(N))
    A,c,f_score = fft_score_A_c(f,N)
    best_f = f.copy(); best_score = f_score
    top = []
    add_top_candidate(top,best_f,best_score)
    # exact
    best_exact = best_f.copy(); best_exact_score = INF
    exact_calls=0; exact_time_used=0.0; last_exact = start
    exact_limit = max(5.0, 0.05*(deadline-start))
    if n <= 25000:
       t0=time.monotonic(); sc=exact_score_array(best_f); dt=...
       exact_time_used += dt
       if finite: best_exact_score=sc; best_exact=best_f.copy(); exact_calls=1

    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
    m_b=np.zeros(n); v_b=np.zeros(n); t_b=0
    F = A.copy(); FR = phase*np.conjugate(A)
    beta_cross=5.0; lr_cross=0.08
    beta_self=5.0; lr_self=0.05
    w_f = log_weights_from_sequence(best_f)
    m_f=np.zeros(n); v_f=np.zeros(n); t_f=0
    stall=0; outer=0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       outer +=1
       # cross inner adaptive
       inner = 2
       if rng.random()<0.2: inner=4
       for _ in range(inner):
           b,w_b,m_b,v_b,t_b = cross_inner_step(F, FR, b, w_b,m_b,v_b,t_b, beta_cross, lr_cross, N)
       # candidate updates
       improved=False
       # evaluate b (t=1) and mix 0.5, maybe 0.25
      _trial_scores=[]
       for tval in (0.5, 1.0, 0.25):
           cand = (1.0 - tval)*best_f + tval*b
           s=cand.sum()
           if s > 0 and finite: cand = cand/s
           sc=fft_score_only(cand,N)
           if finite:
              if sc < f_score:
                  # choose candidate with smallest sc
       # better: keep best_cand
       best_cand=None; best_cand_score=INF
       for tval...:
          ... if sc<best_cand_score: best_cand=cand; best_cand_score=sc
       if best_cand is not None and best_cand_score < best_score:
           best_f=best_cand; best_score=best_cand_score; add_top
           # set current f to best? For cross, f variable track current f? We have best_f and f variables. Let's unify: current f is best_f? In cross, we update best_f directly.
           f = best_f.copy()
           f_score = best_score
           F = np.fft.rfft(f,N); FR=phase*np.conjugate(F)
           # optionally reset b/w
           if rng.random() < 0.6:
               b = f.copy(); w_b=log...; m_b=zeros; v_b=zeros; t_b=0
           else:
               if not finite b: b=f.copy(); ...
               w_b=log_weights_from_sequence(b)
           stall=0; improved=True
       # If best_cand improves current f_score but not global? We use best_f as current, so same.
       if not improved:
           stall += 1
       # self gradient if stall > 10 or sometimes
       if stall > 10 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
           A,c,scur = fft_score_A_c(f,N)
           # if scur < best...
           p=make_weights(c, beta_self)
           # try several lrs

               sc=fft_score_only(cand,N)
               if finite and sc < best_score:
                   best_f=cand; best_score=sc; add_top; f=cand.copy(); f_score=sc; w_f=w_try; improve cross F; reset b=f maybe; stall=0; self_accepted=True; break
           if not self_accepted:
               # random perturbation of global best
               if stall > 30:
                   w_try = w_f + rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, size=n)
                   cand = sequence_from_log_weights(w_try)
                   sc=fft_score_only(cand,N)
                   if finite and sc < best_score * 1.05:
                       # set current f to cand for exploration even if worse? but not update best
                       f=cand.copy(); f_score=sc; w_f=log...; set b=f; reset cross; stall=0; recompute F
                   else:
                       stall=0
       # update betas/lr
       if outer % 100 == 0:
           beta_cross = min(80.0, beta_cross*1.2)
           beta_self = min(100.0, beta_self*1.2)
       # exact evaluation

           if time.monotonic() - last_exact > 30.0 or best_score < ...:

               exact_calls +=1; last_exact=time.monotonic()
               if finite and sc < best_exact_score:

    # ensure final

    else:

        seq=best_exact
    return seq
```

This code has `best_f` as current best/global; in cross update we set f=best_f. So current not separate. The cross variable f should be the current global best, not a possibly worse exploratory. If we do exploratory random when stall, we set f lower score but not best? Need variables carefully.

Better maintain:
- `f`: current sequence for cross/self (may be worse than best for exploration)
- `f_score`: FFT score of f
- `best_f`, `best_score`: validated FFT best
- `w_f` for f.
Cross subproblem uses current f to produce b. Updating f can be to best candidate if it improves best; or to random exploration if stall. Best separate.

In above, I set f=best_f always; no exploration worse. That's okay but may stagnate. To allow exploration, when stall high, set f to perturbation of best (worse), but keep best. Cross will now optimize for this f; if finds improvement to best, update. If not, after some time reset f=best. This allows basins.

Return sequence: Ensure list of Python floats. 
```
def finalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
    a = np.minimum(a,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0.01:
        # If sum too small, scale up (sum1 already)
        if s >0: a *= 0.1/s
        else: a=np.ones(64); s=64
    # ensure sum >0.01
    if a.sum() < 0.01:
       a = a * (0.1 / max(a.sum(), 1e-300))
    # cap again after scale? max <=1000 maybe if a small; if sum large? a sum1 so scale 0.1 no issue.
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

```
def normalize_nonneg(v):
    arr=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not finite:
        return np.ones(max(1,arr.size), dtype=np.float64) / max(1,arr.size)
    return arr/s
```

```
def load_initial_sequence():

    if obj is None: return None
    arr = np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size < 8: return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0); arr=np.maximum(arr,0)
    s=arr.sum()

    arr/=s
    if arr.size > 30000:

    return arr
```

Detailed implementation of `run_search` (mentally write):

```
def run_search(a_start, seed=42, budget_s=2400.0):
    start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 20.0)
    deadline = start + total
    f = normalize_nonneg(a_start)
    n = f.size
    if n < 8:
        f = np.ones(4096)/4096; n=4096
    N = next_fast_len(3*n - 2)
    phase = np.exp(-2j * np.pi * np.arange(N//2 + 1) * (n - 1) / float(N))
    A,c,f_score = fft_score_A_c(f,N)
    A0? no
    best_f = f.copy()
    best_score = f_score
    top=[]
    add_top_candidate(top,best_f,best_score)
    # exact setup
    best_exact = best_f.copy()
    best_exact_score = INF
    exact_time_used = 0.0
    exact_calls = 0
    last_exact = start
    exact_limit = max(10.0, 0.08*total)
    if n <= 25000:
       t0=time.monotonic(); sc=exact_score_array(best_f); exact_time_used += time.monotonic()-t0; exact_calls=1; last_exact=time.monotonic()
       if np.isfinite(sc): best_exact_score=sc; best_exact=best_f.copy()
    # cross init
    b = f.copy()
    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
    m_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t_b = 0
    F = A.copy()
    FR = phase * np.conjugate(A)
    w_f = log_weights_from_sequence(f)
    m_f = np.zeros(n); v_f=np.zeros(n); t_f=0
    beta_cross=5.0; lr_cross=0.08
    beta_self=5.0; lr_self=0.05
    stall=0
    outer=0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        outer += 1
        inner = 2
        if outer % 7 == 0: inner = 4
        for _ in range(inner):
            b,w_b,m_b,v_b,t_b = cross_inner_step(F,FR,b,w_b,m_b,v_b,t_b,beta_cross,lr_cross,N)
        # candidate eval
        prev_f_score = f_score
        best_cand = None; best_cand_score = prev_f_score; best_t = 0.0
        for tval in (0.33, 0.66, 1.0):
            cand = (1.0 - tval) * f + tval * b
            cs = float(np.sum(cand))
            if not np.isfinite(cs) or cs <= 0.0: continue
            cand = cand / cs
            sc = fft_score_only(cand, N)
            if not np.isfinite(sc): continue
            if sc < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score = sc; best_cand = cand; best_t = tval
        if best_cand is not None:
            f = best_cand.copy()
            f_score = best_cand_score
            if f_score < best_score:
                best_score = f_score; best_f = f.copy(); add_top_candidate(top, best_f, best_score)
            if f_score < prev_f_score:
                stall = 0
                lr_cross = min(0.25, lr_cross * 1.05)
            else:
                stall += 1
                lr_cross = max(0.02, lr_cross * 0.9)
            # recompute F FR
            F = np.fft.rfft(f, N)
            FR = phase * np.conjugate(F)
            if best_t >= 0.99:
                b = f.copy()
                w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
                m_b = np.zeros(n); v_b=np.zeros(n); t_b=0
            else:
                if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
                    b = f.copy()
                    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
                    m_b = np.zeros(n); v_b=np.zeros(n); t_b=0
                else:
                    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
        else:
            stall += 1
            lr_cross = max(0.02, lr_cross * 0.95)

        # self attempt
        if (outer % 15 == 0 or stall > 10) and time.monotonic() < deadline:
            A,c,saff = fft_score_A_c(f,N)
            if saff < f_score:
                f_score = saff
                f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64) # maybe
            if saff < best_score:
                best_score = saff; best_f = f.copy(); add_top
            improved_self=False
            for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 2.0):
                cand,w_try,m_f_try,v_f_try,t_f_try = self_gradient_candidate(f,A,c,w_f,m_f,v_f,t_f,beta_self,lr_self*mult,N,phase)
                sc = fft_score_only(cand,N)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < f_score:
                    f = cand.copy(); f_score = sc; w_f = w_try; m_f=m_f_try; v_f=v_f_try; t_f=t_f_try
                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score=sc; best_f=f.copy(); add_top
                    F=np.fft.rfft(f,N); FR=phase*np.conjugate(F)
                    b=f.copy(); w_b=log_weights_from_sequence(b); m_b=np.zeros(n); v_b=np.zeros(n); t_b=0
                    improved_self=True
                    break
            if improved_self:
                stall=0
                lr_self=min(0.2, lr_self*1.05)
            else:
                lr_self=max(0.02, lr_self*0.9)

        # reset/exploration
        if stall > 35:
            if f_score > best_score + 0.05 or rng.random() < 0.3:
                # reset to best
                f = best_f.copy(); f_score = best_score
            else:
                # explore perturbation of best or current
                sig = float(rng.choice((0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4)))
                w_try = log_weights_from_sequence(best_f) + rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=n)
                cand = sequence_from_log_weights(w_try)
                sc = fft_score_only(cand,N)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    f = cand.copy(); f_score = sc
                else:
                    f = best_f.copy(); f_score = best_score
            # after reset, sync w_f, b, F
            w_f = log_weights_from_sequence(f)
            m_f = np.zeros(n); v_f = np.zeros(n); t_f = 0
            b = f.copy()
            w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
            m_b = np.zeros(n); v_b = np.zeros(n); t_b = 0
            F = np.fft.rfft(f,N); FR = phase * np.conjugate(F)
            stall = 0
            # maybe adjust lr
            lr_cross = 0.08

        # betas
        if outer % 60 == 0:
            beta_cross = min(120.0, beta_cross * 1.08 + 0.5)
            beta_self = min(160.0, beta_self * 1.08 + 0.5)

        # exact
        if n <= 25000 and exact_time_used < exact_limit:
            if exact_calls == 0 or time.monotonic() - last_exact > 60.0 or best_score < 1.2:
                t0 = time.monotonic()
                sc = exact_score_array(best_f)
                exact_time_used += time.monotonic() - t0
                exact_calls += 1
                last_exact = time.monotonic()
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
                    best_exact_score = sc
                    best_exact = best_f.copy()
```

Need inside self attempt: When `saff < f_score`, we didn't update f? `f` already same, score computed; no need. But if f_score was old due to changes, update.

When candidate best_cand found, `f_score` may become less than best_score; update best. If f_score improves current but not best, okay. If f_score not improve current but best_cand_score initialized prev_f_score, no best_cand. Good.

`prev_f_score` maybe `f_score` before candidate. If candidate score < prev_f_score, accept. But what if f was worse than best and candidate improves best but not f? impossible if candidate better than best but best<=f? def add_top_candidate(top, a, score, max_items=16):
```
    if not top:
        top.append((score, a.copy()))
        return top
    worst_idx = 0
    worst_score = top[0][0]
    for i in range(1, len(top)):
        if top[i][0] > worst_score:
            worst_score = top[i][0]
            worst_idx = i
    if len(top) < max_items or score < worst_score:
        if len(top) < max_items:
            top.append((score, a.copy()))
        else:
            top[worst_idx] = (score, a.copy())
    return top
```

```
def self_gradient_candidate(f, A, c, w, m, v, t, beta, lr, N, phase):
    n=len(f)
    p=make_weights(c,beta)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    FR=phase*np.conjugate(A)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(P*FR,N)
    grad=2.0*conv[n-1:2*n-1]
    grad_w = f * (grad - np.dot(f, grad))
    return adam_log_step(grad_w,w,m,v,t,lr)
```

Code:
```
def finalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    n = a.size
    scale = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))
    if s > 0:
        a = a * (scale / s)
    else:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * scale / n
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        a = a * (0.01 / max(s, 1e-300))
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```
If n huge, scale maybe >1000 and max clipped, sum maybe lower but >0.01? For n<=12000 scale~154, max maybe <154, no clip. Good.

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative coefficient sequences minimizing

    2 * n * max(np.convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a)^2)

by working in a scale-invariant normalization sum(a)=1.  The optimizer
combines:
  - direct smoothed-Linf descent for the self-convolution,
  - alternately solving/optimizing the convex subproblem min max(f*b)
    used by the previous LP-based method,
  - random block perturbations and restarts,
  - periodic exact numpy.convolve validation.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

EPS = 1e-300
INF = float("inf")
MAX_N = 12000


def next_fast_len(m):
    """Smallest power of two >= m."""
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def normalize_nonneg(v):
    """Return a finite nonnegative vector normalized to sum 1."""
    arr = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        arr = np.ones(max(1, arr.size), dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(arr.sum())
    return arr / s


def log_weights_from_sequence(a):
    """Log of a positive-dimensional affine-core sequence, centered."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, EPS)
    w = np.log(a)
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)
    w -= float(np.max(w))
    return w


def sequence_from_log_weights(w):
    """Stable softmax to a probability vector."""
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if w.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)
    w -= float(np.max(w))
    e = np.exp(w)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(w.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(w.size)
    return e / s


def make_weights(c, beta):
    """Soft weights p_k proportional to exp(beta * (c_k - max(c)) / max(c))."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    L = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(L) or L <= 0.0:
        p = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return p
    x = (c - L) / L
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, -1e300)
    y = np.clip(float(beta) * x, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(y)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return p
    return e / s


def fft_score_only(a, N):
    """FFT-based objective value for a normalized nonnegative sequence."""
    n = len(a)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)
    c = c[: 2 * n - 1]
    if c.size:
        if float(np.min(c)) < 0.0:
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        mx = float(np.max(c))
    else:
        mx = 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return INF
    return float(2.0 * n * mx)


def fft_score_A_c(a, N):
    """Return DFT of a, clipped full self-convolution, and score."""
    n = len(a)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)
    c = c[: 2 * n - 1]
    if c.size:
        if float(np.min(c)) < 0.0:
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        mx = float(np.max(c))
    else:
        mx = 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        score = INF
    else:
        score = float(2.0 * n * mx)
    return A, c, score


def exact_score_array(a):
    """Exact O(n^2) score using numpy.convolve, for final/top candidates."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return INF
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return INF
    b = np.convolve(a, a)
    if b.size == 0:
        return INF
    mx = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return INF
    val = float(2.0 * n * mx / (s * s))
    return val if np.isfinite(val) else INF


def adam_log_step(grad_w, w, m, v, t, lr):
    """One projected-gradient Adam step in log-probability space."""
    t += 1
    beta1 = 0.9
    beta2 = 0.999
    grad_w = np.asarray(grad_w, dtype=np.float64)
    m = m * beta1 + (1.0 - beta1) * grad_w
    gw2 = grad_w * grad_w
    v = v * beta2 + (1.0 - beta2) * gw2
    mhat = m / (1.0 - beta1 ** t)
    vhat = v / (1.0 - beta2 ** t)
    dw = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-12)
    dw = np.where(np.isfinite(dw), dw, 0.0)
    dw = np.clip(dw, -10.0, 10.0)
    w_new = w - float(lr) * dw
    w_new = np.where(np.isfinite(w_new), w_new, 0.0)
    w_new -= float(np.max(w_new))
    a_new = sequence_from_log_weights(w_new)
    return a_new, w_new, m, v, t


def cross_inner_step(F, FR, b, w, m, v, t, beta, lr, N):
    """
    One convex-subproblem step for fixed f:
        minimize max_k (f*b)_k over b >= 0, sum(b)=1.
    """
    n = len(b)
    B = np.fft.rfft(b, N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(F * B, N)
    h = h[: 2 * n - 1]
    if h.size:
        if float(np.min(h)) < 0.0:
            h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
        mx = float(np.max(h))
    else:
        mx = 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
        p = np.zeros(h.size if h.size else 1, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.size:
            p[0] = 1.0
    else:
        p = make_weights(h, beta)

    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    grad = np.fft.irfft(P * FR, N)
    grad = grad[n - 1: 2 * n - 1]
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)

    grad_w = b * (grad - float(np.dot(b, grad)))
    return adam_log_step(grad_w, w, m, v, t, lr)


def self_gradient_candidate(f, A, c, w, m, v, t, beta, lr, N, phase):
    """One smoothed-Linf self-convolution gradient step."""
    n = len(f)
    p = make_weights(c, beta)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    FR = phase * np.conjugate(A)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(P * FR, N)
    grad = 2.0 * conv[n - 1: 2 * n - 1]
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    grad_w = f * (grad - float(np.dot(f, grad)))
    return adam_log_step(grad_w, w, m, v, t, lr)


def add_top_candidate(top, a, score, max_items=16):
    """Keep a small list of best FFT-score candidates."""
    if not top:
        top.append((score, np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
        return top

    worst_idx = 0
    worst_score = float(top[0][0])
    for i in range(1, len(top)):
        si = float(top[i][0])
        if si > worst_score:
            worst_score = si
            worst_idx = i

    if len(top) < max_items or score < worst_score:
        if len(top) < max_items:
            top.append((score, np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
        else:
            top[worst_idx] = (score, np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy())
    return top


def load_initial_sequence():
    """Load the provided SOTA sequence if available."""
    obj = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if obj is None:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None

    if arr.size < 16:
        return None

    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    arr = arr / s

    # Downsample overly long parents to keep the final direct evaluation feasible.
    if arr.size > MAX_N:
        target = 8192
        blocks = int(arr.size) // target
        if blocks > 1:
            use = target * blocks
            arr = np.mean(arr[:use].reshape(target, blocks), axis=1)
        else:
            arr = arr[:target]
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
            arr = arr / s
        else:
            arr = np.ones(target, dtype=np.float64) / float(target)

    if arr.size > MAX_N:
        arr = arr[:MAX_N]
        s = float(arr.sum())
        if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
            arr = arr / s

    return arr


def run_search(a_start, seed=42, budget_s=2400.0):
    """Main hybrid search.  Returns the best sequence found."""
    start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 18.0)
    deadline = start + total_budget

    f = normalize_nonneg(a_start)
    n = f.size
    if n < 16:
        f = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64) / 4096.0
        n = f.size
    if n > MAX_N:
        f = f[:MAX_N]
        f = f / float(np.sum(f))
        n = f.size

    N = next_fast_len(3 * n - 2)
    phase = np.exp(-2j * np.pi * np.arange(N // 2 + 1) * (n - 1) / float(N))

    A, c, f_score = fft_score_A_c(f, N)
    if not np.isfinite(f_score):
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        A, c, f_score = fft_score_A_c(f, N)

    best_f = f.copy()
    best_score = f_score
    top = add_top_candidate([], best_f, best_score)

    # Exact validation bookkeeping.
    best_exact = best_f.copy()
    best_exact_score = INF
    exact_time_used = 0.0
    exact_calls = 0
    last_exact = start
    exact_limit = max(10.0, 0.08 * total_budget)

    if n <= MAX_N:
        t0 = time.monotonic()
        sc = exact_score_array(best_f)
        exact_time_used += time.monotonic() - t0
        exact_calls += 1
        last_exact = time.monotonic()
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            best_exact_score = sc
            best_exact = best_f.copy()

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # If the parent is essentially uniform, break the symmetry immediately.
    if f_score > 1.999:
        best_pert = best_f.copy()
        best_pert_score = f_score
        for _ in range(12):
            sig = float(rng.choice((0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0)))
            w_try = log_weights_from_sequence(best_f) + rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=n)
            cand = sequence_from_log_weights(w_try)
            sc = fft_score_only(cand, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_pert_score:
                best_pert_score = sc
                best_pert = cand.copy()
        f = best_pert.copy()
        f_score = best_pert_score
        if f_score < best_score:
            best_score = f_score
            best_f = f.copy()
            top = add_top_candidate(top, best_f, best_score)

    # Cross-subproblem state: fixed f, variable b.
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, N)
    FR = phase * np.conjugate(F)
    b = f.copy()
    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
    m_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t_b = 0

    # Self-gradient state for current f.
    w_f = log_weights_from_sequence(f)
    m_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t_f = 0

    beta_cross = 5.0
    lr_cross = 0.08
    beta_self = 5.0
    lr_self = 0.05

    stall = 0
    outer = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        outer += 1

        # Safety reset if numeric state becomes invalid.
        if (not np.isfinite(f_score) or
                not np.all(np.isfinite(f)) or
                not np.all(np.isfinite(b))):
            f = best_f.copy()
            f_score = best_score
            w_f = log_weights_from_sequence(f)
            m_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            t_f = 0
            b = f.copy()
            w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
            m_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            t_b = 0
            F = np.fft.rfft(f, N)
            FR = phase * np.conjugate(F)
            stall = 0
            continue

        # --- Alternating convex subproblem min max(f*b) ---
        inner = 2
        if outer % 5 == 0:
            inner = 4
        for _ in range(inner):
            b, w_b, m_b, v_b, t_b = cross_inner_step(
                F, FR, b, w_b, m_b, v_b, t_b, beta_cross, lr_cross, N
            )

        # Try convex combinations of current f and optimized b.
        prev_f_score = f_score
        best_cand = None
        best_cand_score = prev_f_score
        best_t = 0.0

        for tval in (0.33, 0.66, 1.0):
            cand = (1.0 - tval) * f + tval * b
            cs = float(np.sum(cand))
            if not np.isfinite(cs) or cs <= 0.0:
                continue
            cand = cand / cs
            sc = fft_score_only(cand, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_cand_score:
                best_cand_score = sc
                best_cand = cand
                best_t = tval

        if best_cand is not None:
            f = best_cand.copy()
            f_score = best_cand_score

            if f_score < best_score:
                best_score = f_score
                best_f = f.copy()
                top = add_top_candidate(top, best_f, best_score)

            if f_score < prev_f_score:
                stall = 0
                lr_cross = min(0.25, lr_cross * 1.05)
            else:
                stall += 1
                lr_cross = max(0.02, lr_cross * 0.9)

            # Keep log state for self gradients consistent.
            w_f = log_weights_from_sequence(f)
            m_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            t_f = 0

            # Update cross-subproblem fixed sequence.
            F = np.fft.rfft(f, N)
            FR = phase * np.conjugate(F)

            if best_t >= 0.99:
                b = f.copy()
                w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
                m_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                v_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                t_b = 0
            else:
                if not np.all(np.isfinite(b)):
                    b = f.copy()
                    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
                    m_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    v_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    t_b = 0
                else:
                    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
        else:
            stall += 1
            lr_cross = max(0.02, lr_cross * 0.95)

        # --- Periodic direct self-convolution smoothing ---
        if (outer % 15 == 0 or stall > 10) and time.monotonic() < deadline:
            A2, c2, saff = fft_score_A_c(f, N)
            if np.isfinite(saff) and saff < f_score:
                f_score = saff
            if np.isfinite(saff) and saff < best_score:
                best_score = saff
                best_f = f.copy()
                top = add_top_candidate(top, best_f, best_score)

            improved_self = False
            for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 2.0):
                cand, w_try, m_try, v_try, t_try = self_gradient_candidate(
                    f, A2, c2, w_f, m_f, v_f, t_f, beta_self, lr_self * mult, N, phase
                )
                sc = fft_score_only(cand, N)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < f_score:
                    f = cand.copy()
                    f_score = sc
                    w_f = w_try
                    m_f = m_try
                    v_f = v_try
                    t_f = t_try

                    if sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        best_f = f.copy()
                        top = add_top_candidate(top, best_f, best_score)

                    F = np.fft.rfft(f, N)
                    FR = phase * np.conjugate(F)
                    b = f.copy()
                    w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
                    m_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    v_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    t_b = 0
                    improved_self = True
                    break

            if improved_self:
                stall = 0
                lr_self = min(0.20, lr_self * 1.05)
            else:
                lr_self = max(0.02, lr_self * 0.9)

        # --- Restarts / exploration ---
        if stall > 35:
            if f_score > best_score + 0.05 or rng.random() < 0.35:
                f = best_f.copy()
                f_score = best_score
            else:
                sig = float(rng.choice((0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4)))
                w_try = log_weights_from_sequence(best_f) + rng.normal(0.0, sig, size=n)
                cand = sequence_from_log_weights(w_try)
                sc = fft_score_only(cand, N)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    f = cand.copy()
                    f_score = sc
                else:
                    f = best_f.copy()
                    f_score = best_score

            w_f = log_weights_from_sequence(f)
            m_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v_f = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            t_f = 0

            b = f.copy()
            w_b = log_weights_from_sequence(b)
            m_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v_b = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            t_b = 0

            F = np.fft.rfft(f, N)
            FR = phase * np.conjugate(F)

            stall = 0
            lr_cross = 0.08
            lr_self = 0.05

        # Slowly sharpen the smoothed maxima.
        if outer % 60 == 0:
            beta_cross = min(120.0, beta_cross * 1.08 + 0.5)
            beta_self = min(160.0, beta_self * 1.08 + 0.5)

        # --- Exact validation, throttled ---
        if n <= MAX_N and exact_time_used < exact_limit:
            if exact_calls == 0 or time.monotonic() - last_exact > 60.0 or best_score < 1.2:
                t0 = time.monotonic()
                sc = exact_score_array(best_f)
                exact_time_used += time.monotonic() - t0
                exact_calls += 1
                last_exact = time.monotonic()
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
                    best_exact_score = sc
                    best_exact = best_f.copy()

    # Final exact checks for the retained FFT-top candidates.
    if n <= MAX_N:
        for sc_top, a_top in top:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2.0:
                break
            if exact_time_used > exact_limit * 1.5:
                break
            # If the FFT score is already much worse than the exact best, skip.
            if np.isfinite(best_exact_score) and sc_top > best_exact_score + 5e-4:
                continue
            t0 = time.monotonic()
            sc = exact_score_array(a_top)
            exact_time_used += time.monotonic() - t0
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
                best_exact_score = sc
                best_exact = a_top.copy()

    if np.isfinite(best_exact_score):
        return best_exact
    return best_f


def finalize_sequence(a):
    """Scale to sum sqrt(2n), keep valid nonnegative floats, return a list."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)

    n = a.size
    s = float(a.sum())
    scale = float(np.sqrt(2.0 * n))

    if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
        a = (a * scale) / s
    else:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) * (scale / float(n))

    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(a.sum())
    if s2 < 0.01:
        a = a * (0.01 / max(s2, 1e-300))
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    return [float(x) for x in a]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Entry point required by the harness.

    Runs the hybrid FFT/local-search optimizer and returns the best sequence
    found, scaled to sum ~ sqrt(2n) while respecting the evaluator constraints.
    """
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0
    budget = min(max(0.0, budget), 2395.0)

    start = load_initial_sequence()
    if start is None:
        start = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64) / 4096.0

    seq = run_search(start, seed=seed, budget_s=budget - 4.0)
    return finalize_sequence(seq)
```
